ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u32	Dear Marshal! Dear Prime Minister, Mr. Vice Prime Minister! Dear Members of the Government! High Seym! I am particularly pleased to present to the High House the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the governmental draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act and the Act amending the Personal Income Tax Act and other laws, print No 3714. The draft law is part of the historical chronicle of the adoption of the most important laws in the field of public finances, which will reduce the tax burden, as the Prime Minister said, 25 million taxpayers, employees, contractors, [[Applause]] pensioners, as well as entrepreneurs. The tax reduction relates to income generated from the employment relationship, from activities carried out in person, including on the basis of contract orders and works contracts, from the economic activity of natural persons... [[Voice from the room: Cost of obtaining revenue.]] ...from pensions and property rights. There is no change in the tax-free rate of 8 000. zł to 0 zł. The reduction in the tax rate from 18% to 17%, i.e. by 1 percentage point, translates, as the Prime Minister has already said, into a reduction in the tax due by more than 5.5%. In parallel with the reduction in the tax rate, the draft law introduces an increase in revenue costs and thus a reduction in the so-called "tax rate'. a tax wedge, that is, the difference between the amount that the employer spends on the employee employed and the net remuneration, i.e. the remuneration per hand. The costs of obtaining revenue are set out in a flat-rate law. The annual costs of obtaining income from employment relationship, service relationship, cooperative employment relationship and overlay work for one-timers will be increased from PLN 1335 to PLN 3000, i.e. more than twice, and for multi-daters up to PLN 4500. For commuters these amounts are increased to PLN 3600 and PLN 5400 respectively. With a minimum wage of PLN 2250 an annual profit for the taxpayer will amount to nearly PLN 500, exactly PLN 472, and with the average monthly gross wage forecast for the social economy of PLN 4765 an annual profit for the taxpayer will amount to over PLN 700, exactly PLN 732. It is worth noting that in 2019 the minimum wage was increased to 2500 PLN and its increase compared to 2008 is more than twice. This was not accompanied by an increase in revenue costs for employees. This bill changes things after 11 years. This double reduction in income tax for millions of taxpayers makes this historical project in the field of tax policy of the Polish government. Today, it is worth mentioning, as the Prime Minister also mentioned, that it was in the rule of law and justice in 2005 and 2007 that the tax wedge was lowered by the Minister of Finance, Prof. Zyta Gilowska. Thus, the Law and Justice Government gradually improves the material situation of Polish families by widely accepted multiple social programs, improves the situation of 25 million taxpayers by lowering the tax on individuals, including abolishing tax for 2 million young people, until 26 years old, [[♪ The bell ♪]] reducing the tax for CIT corporates. [[Applause]] On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I declare that we will support this project in further work. And as chairman of the Committee on Public Finance, I ask all Members' Clubs to adopt this bill, which is important to all taxpayers, beyond political divisions. We proceed with the examination of item 24 of the agenda: Report of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the Government's draft law amending the Water Law Act and certain other laws (prints No 3695 and 3741). I ask Mr Jerzy Materna to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u84	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Water Law and certain other laws, prints No 3695 and 3741. The Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation discussed the draft law at its meeting on 8 August 2019. The Commission adopted seven amendments tabled by Members of the Law and Justice and minor amendments tabled by the Legislative Office, which were clear, orderly and editorial. The entire bill was passed by the committee without a vote of objection. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment to the Act Water Law will allow funding from the state budget of the activities of state services: the State hydrological and meteorological services, the State hydrogeological service and the State security services of constructions. The State Meteorological Service shall perform the State's tasks in the field of hydrological and meteorological coverage of society, the environment, cultural heritage, the economy and the recognition of threats to dangerous phenomena occurring in the atmosphere or the hydrosphere, as well as for the purposes of shaping and protecting the water resources of the country. The State Hydrogeological Service shall perform the State's tasks for the purposes of the identification, balancing and protection of groundwater, carrying out ongoing analyses and evaluation of the hydrogeological situation. Most of the tasks carried out by the service are continuous work which cannot be interrupted or suspended. The efficient functioning of the hydrological-meteorological shield and the hydrogeological shield of the country is an integral part of national security. Currently, the costs cover Polish Waters, but the experience and analyses carried out so far show that services should have a stable source of funding, i.e. State budget. It should be independent of the financial results of the National Water Farm of Poland. The funding of these entities from the State budget would take place from 1 January 2020. The draft law also amends the provisions determining the content of the preliminary assessment of the environmental status of marine waters. The amendment is necessary in view of the need to implement the provisions of Commission Directive (EU) 2017/845. The new requirements introduced by the Marine Water Environmental Assessment Directive are the need to determine the extent of each habitat lost in square kilometres or per cent as a percentage of the total habitat range. The proposed amendments, resulting from the transposition of Annex III to the Directive, extend the catalogue of human activities affecting the state of the marine environment, specifying those to be applied in the socio-economic analysis carried out by the Minister for Maritime Affairs and in the lists of pressures prepared by the Minister for Water Management, the Minister for Maritime Affairs and the Minister for Fisheries. The project assumes that the ownership rights of the State Treasury in relation to inland waterways of particular transport importance are to be exercised by the State Water Farm of Poland. This means transferring ownership rights from the Minister for Inland Navigation to the Polish Waters in relation to inland waterways of particular transport importance. Currently, tasks in the exercise of the State Treasury's ownership rights vis-à-vis inland waterways are scattered between two entities, namely: the State Water Farm of Poland and the Minister responsible for inland waterway transport. Due to the acquisition in 2018 by the Minister of Maritime and Inland Navigation of the Department of Government Administration: water management, this body has obtained appropriate supervisory powers and thus has an impact on the exercise by the State Water Farm of Poland of ownership rights on inland waterways. Solutions have been proposed to improve administrative procedures, in particular as regards requirements for the issue of water legislation, as well as decisions defining the nature of waters setting the shoreline, exempting from compliance with bans in areas of particular danger of flooding. With the introduction of new regulations, it is expected to accelerate the issue of water legislation, including in cases requiring notification, i.e. in silent consent. The maximum water intake unit and the discharge of waste water in water law permits issued before 1 January 2018 are planned to be clarified for the purpose of establishing a fixed fee for water services. The provisions in force require that the fixed fee be calculated on the basis of the quantity of storage/drop in cubic metres per second specified in the water law permit. Since licences issued before the entry into force of the current Water Law do not specify the maximum collection/drop in cubic metres per second but in other units, it is necessary to clarify which of these units should be converted into cubic metres per second. Finally, Madam Marshal, High Izbo, Minister, as rapporteur, I recommend the adoption of this draft law amending the Water Law Act and certain other laws. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u86	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government's draft Print Act No. 3695 and 3741. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 2018 was the beginning of a wide-ranging reform of water management. In January 2018, the National Water Farm of Poland was established, which took over the tasks carried out so far by the National Water Management Board, regional water management management boards, as well as the tasks of the Marshals of voivodships related to the maintenance of water and other property of the State Treasury related to water management as well as investments in water management. National Water Farm Polish is a huge institution consisting of: National Water Management Board, 11 regional water management boards, 50 water catchment management boards and 330 water surveillance authorities. The employment rate at the end of 2018 was 5719 employees. The first few months of the new institution's operation were linked to a number of challenges. Polish waters act as a regulator of fees for water supply and waste water discharge. In 2018, Polish waters approved 2570 tariffs for collective water supply and collective sewage disposal and gave an opinion on 1860 draft regulations for water supply and waste water discharge. Polish waters guard the prices of water and sewage, blocking any unjustified increases. Polish waters perform a number of investment tasks which are crucial for the safety of the flood country and the improvement of water resources. In 2018 Polish waters carried out 137 investment tasks, among others. among the priority investments was the completion of the water level in Malczyce. The investment process related to the construction of the water level below Sierzewo on Vistula and Lubież and Ścinawa on Oder has begun and is implemented according to the schedule. Other important investments carried out by the Polish Waters include modernization of the Wrocław Water Node, construction of ice-breakers for RZGW Gdańsk, Szczecin, Warsaw, comprehensive flood protection Żuław, continuation of activities related to the construction of the flood tank Racibórz Dolny on the river Oder in the Silesian Voivodeship. Polish waters implement or plan to carry out 91 investments in the near future, one of the tasks of which is to increase water retention. The purchase of specialist equipment and equipment necessary for carrying out statutory tasks of Polish Waters was carried out for almost 39 million PLN. Polish waters prepare a number of strategic planning documents. They prepare, among others. the draft update of flood risk management plans, plans are being drawn up to counter the effects of drought, which aim to reduce the incidence of this phenomenon and increase the volume of water reclaimed in Poland. In August 2019 the information campaign "Stop droughts!" was launched, combined with consultations of this document. The Ministry of Water Management and Inland Shipping prepared a second amendment of the Water Law Act. Today's draft law aims in particular to transpose the revision of the Marine Directive and to ensure stable financing of services: the State hydrological and meteorological services, the State hydrogeological service and the State security services of building building building building. An important element is also the improvement of administrative procedures or the elimination of further interpretative doubts. This is a good step towards better management of water management in Poland. Here, the Law and Justice Club proposes an amendment which aims to increase the permitted depth of joints from 2 m to 3 m, which will be carried out on the basis of a water legal application. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the bill and will vote in favour of its adoption. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u106	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club Kukiz'15, I have the honour to present a position regarding a government draft law amending the Water Law Act and some other laws, print no. 3695 and 3741. This project aims to implement European Union law and concerns inter alia.: ensure a stable source of funding for the State services referred to in the Water Law Act, by financing from the State budget, changes in the regulation of environmental objectives, monitoring of waters, reporting on the implementation of the National Urban Waste Water Treatment Programme, the rules on fertilization plans and the plan against drought effects. As of January 2018, the National Water Farm of Poland was established, [[Polish waters]] which is a public legal person within the meaning of the Law of 27 August 2009 on public finances, although problems and inaccuracies still arise in the current legal situation in connection with the operation of the Polish Waters. The aim of the bill is to provide a stable source of funding for the State services referred to in the Water Law Act (PSHM - State Hydrological and Metheorological Service, PSH - State Hydrogeological Service, PSdsBBP - State Security Service for the Construction of Five-Fertering Buildings), in order to provide these services with financial resources for the performance of statutory tasks. The recommended solution is the financing from the state budget of the activities mentioned above. State services. Currently, the operating costs of these services are covered by the National Water Farm of Poland. However, the analyses carried out show that providing them with a stable source of funding, independent of the financial results of the PGW WP, requires the cost of their operation to be covered by the State budget. The finalisation of these entities from the State budget would take place from 1 January 2020. In addition, due to the planned transfer to the Polish Waters of competence in the exercise of the property rights of the State Treasury in relation to inland waterways of particular transport importance, specific tasks in this respect were assigned to the relevant units within the structure of Polish Waters. Therefore, in the current state of fact, the concentration of ownership rights for inland waterways within the Polish Waters is an appropriate solution to take into account the different interests that should be maintained by the introduction of appropriate regulations. The contested law also amends the provisions setting out the content of the preliminary assessment of the environmental status of marine waters, it is necessary in view of the need to implement the provisions of Directive 2017/845/EU, which introduced the requirements for the determination of the natural habitat coverage and extended the catalogue of human activities affecting the state of the marine environment. It's about actions that have, among others. use in socio-economic analyses. The project also introduces an extension of the reporting process related to the implementation of the National Urban Waste Water Treatment Scheme. The Act reduces by half the time the municipalities had, agglomerations for the preparation and transmission of the report on the implementation of KPOŚK for a given year. At the same time it introduced a deadline for verification, preparation and transmission by the Polish Waters of an annual report taking into account all agglomerations - now there are 1587. In practice, it is pointed out that 6 months of work is needed to prepare a report, including data verification. Another change is to allow local authorities to finance investments in water management to a wider extent than is the case under current regulations. Local government units are controlled by regional clearing houses, which carefully verify the legality of the resolutions taken. Amendment of Article 238 enables local authorities to participate in investment and maintenance costs, among others. flood shafts. The next proposal concerns water law permits, where the maximum amount of water to be collected and waste water to be introduced will be determined in cubic metres per second. The point is that the fees for water services set out in water law permits issued before 1 January 2018 were determined in m3 per hour and should be converted in m3 per second for the calculations to be carried out. These parameters are necessary because they are part of the formula used to calculate the fixed levy for the collection of water, the discharge of water into water and the introduction of sewage into water or land. This regulation is due to enter into force from 2020. It is also extremely important to improve administrative procedures. The aim is to clarify the requirements for the issue of water legislation, as well as decisions: defining the nature of the waters setting the shoreline, exempting from bans in areas of particular danger of flooding. By introducing new regulations, the result will be speeding up the issue of water regulations, even in cases requiring notification, i.e. in silent consent mode. To sum up, we believe that this law introduces a number of positive solutions. Therefore, the Kukiz Parliamentary Club15 supports this draft law.

According to the current legal situation, employment on board fishing vessels is regulated by the Law of 5 August 2015 on Maritime Labour, but in view of the different working specificities, these provisions apply only to a limited extent. ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u110	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice, I will respond to the Act on Work on Fishing Ships and present an opinion on it. Namely, the bill deals with working conditions and life on ships, including on fishing vessels. It is currently governed by the Law of 5 August 2015 on Maritime Labour. This law implements into the national legal order the provisions of the Maritime Labour Convention adopted by the General Conference of the International Labour Organisation in Geneva on 23 February 2006, called MLC. This Convention shall not apply to fishing vessels, therefore in accordance with Article. Point 2 (7) of the Law on Maritime Labour These vessels shall be considered as non-conventional vessels and the provisions of the Act shall apply to them only to a limited extent. Due to the specific nature of fishing activities on board fishing vessels, the provisions of the Maritime Labour Act do not adequately regulate the working relationship on such vessels. And that's the problem. It has been repeatedly stressed by the social partners, so we meet social expectations. In addition to the implementation of EU legislation, the aim of the proposed law is above all to regulate comprehensively in a separate legal act issues relating to life and work on board fishing vessels. The draft law therefore comprehensively regulates the rights and obligations of the parties to the employment relationship on Polish-owned fishing vessels, the mediation of work for those seeking work on these vessels, the requirements concerning working documents on fishing vessels, working conditions and the life of fishermen on Polish-owned fishing vessels, and the protection of the health and social protection of fishermen. At the same time, the project implements the international standards on working relations on fishing vessels set out in Convention No 188 on Work in the Fisheries Sector, adopted by the General Conference of the International Labour Organisation in Geneva on 30 May 2007, as it introduces an obligation for fishing vessels of more than 24 metres of length to have a document confirming compliance with the requirements of this Convention as regards the living and working conditions of fishermen. The draft Act on ratification of Convention 188 is currently being examined, actually it was, just today, at the meeting of the Joint Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, we discussed it, and it was also attended by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs. The Convention was transposed into European Union law by Council Directive of 19 December 2016 implementing the Agreement on the implementation of the International Labour Organisation Convention on Labour in the Fisheries Sector of 2007 concluded on 21 May 2012 between the General Confederation of Agricultural Cooperatives to the European Union, the European Transport Workers' Federation and the Associations of National Business Organisations in the Fisheries Sector in the European Union. Thus, the proposed law also carries out, and maybe above all, the transposition of the above mentioned law. directives. The Directive entered into force on 16 November 2017 and should be implemented in the national legal order by 15 November 2019. The solutions introduced will result in improved working and safety conditions on fishing vessels and the lives of fishermen, but in order to get the records straightened out, I myself, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, submit an amendment to the article. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4. The wording of the Act on Conditions of Employment and Organisation of Work on a Fishing Vessel referred to in Chapter 5 shall not apply to fishing vessels of less than 12 metres in length, except for the provision of Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3. The purpose of the amendment is to correct the erroneous reference in Article. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 draft law on work on fishing vessels. Currently Art. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 wrongly refers to Article. Article 32 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 3, whereas it should refer to Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 projects. The intention of reference in Article. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 4th project requires fishermen to provide free food and drinking water also on fishing vessels of less than 12 metres in length. Such an obligation shall be provided for in Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 projects. As I pointed out at the outset, the amendment is only intended to correct the wrong reference. On your hands, Marshal, I make this amendment.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u125	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Sorry I'm late. I would like to present in a concise manner the report of the Committee on Infrastructure relating to the government draft law amending the law on rail transport, contained in print No 3668. I would just like to say at the outset that the aim of the project is to implement the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 13 June 2018. In that judgment, including the judgment established by the Court of Justice of the European Union, the Court of Justice of the European Union found that the Republic of Poland infringed EU law by failing to take the necessary measures to guarantee the organisational and decision-making independence of the investigating body, i.e. The State Commission for the Investigation of Railway Accidents, from the railway infrastructure manager, such as Polskie Linie Kolejowe Spółka Akcyjna, over which the owner supervision is exercised on behalf of the State Treasury by the Minister responsible for transport. The Court divided the position of the committee which lodged the complaint in this case by accusing Poland that the relationship in which the Minister responsible for Transport operates the State Commission for the Investigation of Railway Accidents carrying out tasks on behalf of the Minister responsible for Transport and being a formal organisational unit serving him, while the minister responsible for the supervision of the ownership of the Polish Railway Lines, violates the principle of independence of the national investigating body from the infrastructure manager. This state of affairs in the Court of Justice's assessment may result in conflicts of interest because the minister responsible for transport, when exercising ownership supervision over Polish Railway Lines, may influence the work carried out by the Railway Accident Investigation Commission in such a way that the Polish investigating body does not show any violations or errors of the PKP PLK which could have caused the railway event in question. In view of the content of this decision, the amendment regulates the exclusion of the State Commission for Railway Accident Investigation from the organisational structure of the Ministry of Infrastructure and subsequently its inclusion in the organisational structure of another public administration. In the light of this, of course, of the earlier ruling, the Minister responsible for Transport should be deprived of the competence to appoint and dismiss the composition of that committee. This is the consequence of this decision and regulation, which was the subject of the committee's work. During the committee's work, the content of the amendment to the Railway Transport Act was supplemented by one amendment clarifying the status of members of the State Railway Accident Investigation Commission. ad hoc members and certain drafting changes were made. Other amendments, other changes other than those I just mentioned, were not made to the bill. So much for me as rapporteur. Thank you for your attention, Marshal. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u126	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Piotr King, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak. Please, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u127	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present our position on the government draft law amending the Law on Rail Transport, Seym Printing No 3668 and 3767 respectively. First of all, it should be stressed that the aim of the project is to implement the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 13 June 2018. In its judgment, the Court of Justice of the European Union found an infringement of Union law by failing to take the measures necessary to guarantee the organisational and decision-making independence of the investigating body, the State Commission for the Investigation of Railway Accidents. In this case, it is about separating the commission from the infrastructure manager, PKP PLK SA, over which ownership supervision is exercised on behalf of the State Treasury by the Minister responsible for transport. The Court of Justice divided the position of the European Commission, which lodged a complaint in this case, accusing Poland that the relationship in which the State Commission for the Investigation of Rail Accidents performs tasks on behalf of the Minister responsible for Transport and is formally an organisational unit serving it, the Ministry of Infrastructure, while the Minister responsible for the supervision of the owner transport of PKP PLK, at the same time, violates the principle of independence of the national investigating body from the infrastructure manager. According to the Court of Justice, such a state of affairs may give rise to a conflict of interest, since the Minister responsible for Transport, when exercising ownership supervision over PKP PLK, may influence the work of the railway accident investigation committee in such a way that the Polish investigating body does not show any breaches or errors of PKP PLK which could result in a given railway event. Given the content of the decision of the Court of Justice, and in particular the need for its urgent implementation by the Republic, there is a need to exclude the State Commission for the Investigation of Railway Accidents from the organisational structure of the Ministry of Infrastructure and subsequently to include it in the organisational structure of another public administration office. In the light of that judgment, the Minister responsible for Transport should be deprived of the competence to appoint and remove the composition of the State Commission for the Investigation of Railway Accidents and to dispose of funds allocated to the operation of the Commission. Those powers shall be delegated to another body. High Seym! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice intends to support this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u128	Thank you, sir. Mr Stanisław Lamczyk, Parliamentary Club Civic Platform - Civic Coalition. Please, sir.

However, the question arises as to whether it is a good decision to place it in the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration. It is a judgment of the Court of the European Union. The Civic Platform is in agreement with the separation of railway accident investigation committees within the structures of the Ministry of Infrastructure. Secondly, there are doubts about the mouth. 10 and as regards point 5 added to it, which states that a member of the committee does not provide work or services to the manager, railway operator or user of the rail sidewalk, etc. Here we have to count on the fact that people working in the committee thus have blocked access to work with foreign rail carriers. In view of the forthcoming opening of the railway market, point 5 should be clarified, adding to it: operating in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club is in favour of transferring these competences outside the Ministry of Infrastructure. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u131	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on a government draft law amending the Environmental Law. The draft is contained in print No 3711, and the committee's report is in print No 3772. This draft was addressed to the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry on 31 July. Yesterday, 29 August, the committee, following its first reading, began a detailed examination of the project. The result is a print report 3772 presented by me. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This draft Print Act No 3711 transposes into the Polish legal order Commission Directive 2015/996 of 19 May 2015 establishing common methods for noise assessment in accordance with Directive 2002/49/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 June 2002 relating to the assessment and management of environmental noise levels. In addition, the proposal removes shortcomings in the transposition and application of the second of these directives, i.e. 2002/49/EC. The European Commission, in its call to remedy the deficiencies, concluded, inter alia, that the obligation to draw up environmental programmes against noise only applies to areas where noise levels exceed the permissible level, as stated in the Article. Article 119 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Environmental law, because the provisions of the Directive are being implemented under this law - and this obligation should also apply to areas where noise levels are not exceeded, as well as silent areas. There is also no regulation regarding the transmission to the European Commission before each round of the production of acoustic maps and environmental protection programme against noise data on agglomerations, roads, railway lines and airports whose operation results in increased noise and for which it is required to draw up acoustic maps. There is also some fragmentation of the competences of the bodies responsible for the preparation of acoustic maps. So far these maps have not been drawn up in time - we can even talk about the 2012 and 2013 arrears. - and the Minister for the Environment had no tools, no instruments to discipline those responsible for carrying out the tasks resulting from the implementation of the Directives. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Now let me briefly talk about the changes introduced by this project. For the correct implementation of Commission Directive 2015/996, a body drawing up strategic noise maps shall be required to apply the noise assessment methods set out in the Annex to that Directive. In order to fully transpose Directive 2002/49/EC, firstly, the obligation to draw up plans and programmes is extended to areas where noise levels are not exceeded and to quiet areas. In addition, the definition of noise indicators for reference to ISO standard 1996 In addition, the Chief Environmental Inspector will be responsible for providing the European Commission with data identifying agglomerations above 100,000. residents, data on major roads, railway lines and airports - these are objects for which common sense suggests that they can generate increased noise levels. In accordance with the regulations we are introducing, the Chief Environmental Inspector will provide the European Commission with data from strategic noise maps and summaries of programmes developed and transmitted by the Marshals of voivodships; data on cities above 100,000. citizens, main roads, railway lines and airports will be transferred to the General Environmental Inspector by city presidents and managers of these facilities, because the regulations we make require these entities to be transferred. The programmes will be drawn up exclusively by the Marshals of voivodships, and therefore there will be 16 programmes drawn up in a uniform manner and evaluated by these entities, i.e. presidents of cities over 100,000. residents and entities that manage major roads, main railway lines and lines in agglomeration areas above 100,000. residents, as well as major airports and airports affecting agglomerations above 100,000. residents. In addition, the draft introduces penalties for the timely transmission of data. These are quite severe punishments: 1 000. PLN per day when drawing up strategic noise maps - but let me remind you, there we are dealing with backlogs from 2013. - also for the timely transmission of data from strategic maps and the timely transmission of summary programmes. The heights of these penalties are, of course, set out in the draft law. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In the course of the work of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, nine substantive amendments were introduced to the draft, of which amendments 1, 3, 5, and 7 were voted jointly. They consisted in clarifying the provisions concerning the location on forms made available in the Public Information Bulletin of the Chief Environmental Inspector for the transmission of identification data, data from strategic noise maps and summary of the environmental programme against noise. In general, I must say here that the Act is of such a strict technical nature, because this is precisely the very technical provisions of the two directives to which we refer and which the proposal implements. High Izbo! Other substantive amendments concern the removal of interpretation doubts, e.g. whether a strategic noise map should be drawn up every 5 years or only updated, including a noise protection programme. Such interpretative doubts, when it comes to understanding the rules, eliminate these amendments. It's also about adding. One amendment makes it possible to add the voivodship as the body required to monitor and correct the expenditure limit for the implementation of the law, which is set out in the expenditure rule. Also in one of the last articles of the Act we are dealing with the establishment of a limit on expenditure for the 10th anniversary related to the implementation of this law. There is quite a large part of the transitional provisions, because, as I say, the situation in this area is quite complicated and you need different cases from the different stages of the implementation of these obligations, which the law imposes, somehow, on the new rules. I would also like to inform you that 28 legislative amendments have been introduced into the text and that the whole of the project has been adopted without votes against and abstentions. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I kindly ask the High House to adopt a government draft law amending the Environmental Law, which is contained in the report submitted by the Committee on Printing No 3772. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Therefore, we have given the committee in this project also the right to order the prosecution to carry out activities that have not been carried out, i.e. not only to monitor how these prosecutorial investigations are conducted, not only to respond to the conclusion that the investigation is not conducted as it should be conducted, that there is a chronicity, that certain activities are not carried out, but also to the situation where, for example, the prosecutor will decide that he will not conduct proceedings, because the case has been statute-barred, but also to the prosecution or to indicate to the prosecution that he should carry out all proceedings to determine the facts, also to indicate the need for specific actions to be taken. The committee will have such a right, so it has absolutely full opportunity to determine the facts effectively, identify the perpetrator, and then bring the matter to an end, to the finality of placing the data of the perpetrator in the pedophile register. ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u245	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed on 19 August 2019 the above draft law to the Committee on Infrastructure for First Reading. The Infrastructure Commission, after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 29 August 2019, asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. It is worth recalling that the draft law amending the Postal Law and the Telecommunications Law aims to enable the correct application of Regulation 2018/644 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 18 April 2018 on cross-border parcel delivery services. In particular, the draft fulfils the obligation imposed on Member States in Article. 8 Regulation 2018/644, which requires Member States to impose sanctions on parcel delivery service providers for failure to comply with the Regulation, i.e. the obligation to provide the national regulatory authority of the Member State with the information referred to in Article. 4 and Article 4 Article 6 (6) (a) 5 Regulation 2018/644 and transmission to that authority in accordance with Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Regulation 2018/644 public price lists for the delivery of single postal consignments not in correspondence. The penalties for failure to comply with the requirements laid down in the Regulation are similar to those imposed on postal operators for failing to comply with the reporting obligations referred to in Article. 43 the Law of 23 November 2012 on postal law and the provisions of Article. 128 of that law, specifying the method of calculating the penalty in the case of post office entities which have not provided the UKE President with the data necessary to determine the basis for the assessment of the penalty imposed in accordance with Article. 126. High Seym! The Committee on Infrastructure requests the Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Thank you very much.

In particular, this draft fulfils the obligations imposed on Member States in Article. 8 Regulation 2018/644, which requires Member States to impose sanctions on parcel delivery service providers for failure to comply with the Regulation, i.e. the obligation to provide the national regulatory authority of the Member State with the information referred to in Article. 4 and Article 4 Article 6 (6) (a) 5 Regulation 2018/644 and transmission to that authority in accordance with Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Regulation 2018/644 public price lists for the delivery of single postal consignments not in correspondence. The penalties for failure to comply with the requirements laid down in the Regulation are similar to those imposed on postal operators for failing to comply with the reporting obligations referred to in Article. 43 the Law of 23 November 2012 on postal law and the provisions of Article. 128 of that law, specifying the method of calculating the penalty in the case of post office entities which have not provided the UKE President with the data necessary to determine the basis for the assessment of the penalty imposed in accordance with Article. 126. Mr. Marshal! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice intends to support this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u249	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - Civic Coalition on the governmental draft law amending the Postal Law Act and the Telecommunications Law after the presentation of the report of the committee contained in print No 3769. High Izbo! The draft law on amending the Postal Law and the Telecommunications Law submitted by the Government aims to enable the correct application of Regulation 2018/644 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 18 April 2018 on cross-border parcel delivery services. This Regulation is an attempt to create new conditions for the provision of these services throughout the Union. It stresses the availability of public price lists for parcel delivery and enhances the transparency of these price lists. It creates new competences of national regulatory authorities for market monitoring and regulatory oversight of parcel delivery service providers. The positive effects of the implementation of this Regulation will be on individuals, micro-entrepreneurs, small entrepreneurs as well as persons with disabilities and persons with reduced mobility. It is particularly important for the development of e-commerce. For this sector of the economy, excessive parcel delivery prices are a major barrier to growth. According to the Recital Regulation, improving transparency and price comparability of cross-border parcel delivery services should help to reduce excessive differences in fees, including differences for domestic and cross-border services. The Regulation shall apply directly, but in accordance with the wording of the Article. 4, Article Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 6 (6) (a) 5 ww. Regulations parcel delivery service providers and single parcel delivery service providers are obliged to provide the competent authorities, and in Poland it is the President of UKE, with the information listed in that Regulation. This includes: the volume of turnover, the number of parcels, sub-contractor data, price lists or information on how the fees are to be set. Member States pursuant to Article. 8 Regulations are required to lay down sanctions for failure to comply with the abovementioned provisions. the obligation to inform the European Commission of the adoption of national rules. The draft law is therefore implemented as defined in the provision of the article. 8 the obligation to impose sanctions. According to the government's proposal, the amount of the fine will not exceed 2% income or, if the entity has not been established or has not obtained income, or has been operating for less than 12 months, the penalty may be 500 000. Euros. It will be a reference to punishment. It should be noted that similar penalties for failure to comply with the information obligation are already covered by existing legislation, inter alia. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 126 or Art. 128 Postal rights. In addition, the parcel delivery service provider, which has not yet provided information, will be required to do so by 10 January 2020. The draft also proposes that for the purposes of setting the threshold from which the information obligation begins and 50 persons should be included in the Regulation, persons working for subcontractors of operators. This provision is optional. On the other hand, the government's inclusion of this proposal in the project justified the nature of the Polish market. Therefore, the question becomes obvious, the important question: Is this a proper assessment and does not the provision proposed by the government cause problems for our Polish companies that provide service services? It is not hard to imagine that these additional administrative obligations for small companies will be burdensome. If it turned out that in Europe only our Polish companies have such responsibilities, the government would put them at an extreme disadvantage. I therefore draw attention to the need to carefully identify the state of implementation of the Directive in other Member States. Therefore, Minister, on behalf of the Civic Platform Club - Civic Coalition, I request that the government provide full information on the scope of implementation of the directive in other Member States, for example. Germany, the Czech Republic, Slovakia, France, Austria and Italy. This will allow, I would say, such a fundamental assessment of whether the regulations proposed by the government will not cause problems in our small Polish companies providing such services. ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u281	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Senate draft law amending the Act on Recognizing as invalid judgments handed down against people being repressed for the purpose of the independent existence of the Polish State after the work on this project in committee and subcommittee. Until now, the Act was properly repressed only by persons to whom it was decided to intern in connection with the introduction of martial law in Poland on 13 December 1981, and by persons who, between 1 November 1982 and 28 February 1983, carried out an active military service to which they were appointed as an activity for the independent existence of the Polish State. Moreover, the possibility of payment of compensation and compensation depended on the actual implementation of the decision, the damage resulting from its very adoption was ignored. Today's amendment extends the catalogue of persons entitled to claim compensation as damage not only to enforcement but also to the issuing of certain decisions or decisions. In addition, a period of up to 10 years is also extended during which, after the annulment of the decision, claims may be sought. The emphasis in my opinion requires that the proposed legislation continues to overlook some of the people being repressed. They do not include persons who were injured by the use of other institutions provided for in the Code of Criminal Procedure of 19 April 1969, i.e. detained, temporarily arrested or deprived of liberty in connection with criminal proceedings pending against them, who are on psychiatric follow-up at a medical facility in connection with criminal proceedings pending against them or for which an order has been issued to seek an alert. Nevertheless, I believe that these changes are worthy of support. They refer to the order of the Constitutional Tribunal of 2 December 2014 - sygn. Act: SK 7/14 - and the specific guidelines which the Court has formulated for the legislator. The Court stressed that there were no powers so called. A positive legislator and left this matter - which he considered to be a legislative omission - to regulate Parliament. At the same time, he pointed out that the legislator's inaction in this area was gross, as earlier rulings suggested the need to ensure legal coherence in this matter and to take full account of historical circumstances. As pointed out by the Constitutional Tribunal in the judgment cited above: "The very adoption of the decision on internment was, of course, an act of combating the opposition by means of legal mechanisms and had negative consequences for the personal and professional lives of those repressed (...)." The Free and Solidarni Circle supports this bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u305	Thank you. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to present the Government's bill on the repeal of the Act on special hydrocarbon tax and on the amendment of certain other laws which provides for the repeal of the Act of 25 July 2014 on special hydrocarbon tax and at the same time changes the time limit for taxation of extraction of natural gas and oil on the basis of the provisions of the Act of 2 March 2012 on the tax on the extraction of certain minerals. It should be recalled that the Act on special hydrocarbon tax was established at a time when high stocks of non-conventional gas were forecasted to exist in Poland. The most optimistic forecast on shale gas resources in Poland was published in April 2011 by the Energy Information Administration, which is the analytical and statistical arm of the American energy department. According to the EIA report, which assessed the potential of shale gas resources in 32 countries, deposits in Poland were to be the largest in Europe and cover 5.3 trillion m3 of gas. Expecting a great interest of investors, including foreign investors, in the activity of exploration and exploitation of shale gas deposits, while at the same time seeking to provide the Polish state with the opportunity to participate in profits from shale gas extraction, the Polish Sejm passed a law on 25 July 2014 on a special hydrocarbon tax. Later very detailed analyses of shale gas resources in the Baltic-sublasco-lubular basin, including those carried out by the National Geological Institute, were not so optimistic. The interest of potential investors in exploration and extraction of natural gas, including unconventional resources, has also decreased significantly. According to information issued by the Ministry of Environment on 30 April 2014, there are 20 concessions in Poland for the exploration of shale gas, which means that, compared to the situation as at 1 July 2013, which was assessed for the purpose of assessing the effects of the regulation in relation to the Act on special hydrocarbon tax when 108 concessions were issued in Poland, the number of licences in force decreased by 81%. It is also noticeable that the dynamics of exploration and exploration work in the field of gas from shale formations have decreased dramatically. For this reason, the current assumptions behind the introduction of a special hydrocarbon tax are no longer valid. Currently, a special hydrocarbon tax, which according to Article. The 35th Act is to be paid on revenues obtained from 1 January 2020, potentially affects a small number of entities, mainly with the participation of the State Treasury involved in the production of hydrocarbons, for which the main source of this raw material remains conventional natural gas deposits located in Poland and abroad. This situation is therefore different from the conditions adopted at the stage of the act, when it was assumed that the fees charged to a large number of entities, including foreign ones, would operate in the field of hydrocarbon mining in Poland. As a result of maintaining a special hydrocarbon tax, vertically integrated companies will need to be divided into natural gas traders and operators, and this will involve a number of long-term and costly activities. The maintenance of a specific hydrocarbon tax would therefore entail negative social, economic, high costs for restructuring operations of natural gas extraction and gas trading entities, while, as it is evident from the tax returns submitted by the taxpayers of the SPD, it is doubtful that the state budget will be able to obtain significant tax revenue in the future. In the view of the Government, it is therefore appropriate to repeal as soon as possible the provisions of the Act of 25 July 2014 on a special hydrocarbon tax, while taking into account any revenue generated by operators extracting gas from deposits with a non-conventional tax on the extraction of certain minerals under the Law of 2 March 2012 on the extraction of certain minerals. Notwithstanding the repeal of the Act on special hydrocarbon tax, the project contains a number of amendments aimed at simplifying CIT tax for taxable persons, in particular those not engaged in economic activities and income exempt from income tax. The first of these changes, in Article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the CIT Act, concerns the obligation for non-business taxable persons to submit their accounts. CIT taxpayers who are not entrepreneurs, like other CIT taxpayers, are now required to draw up non-structured electronic accounts and submit them to the competent tax authorities. In turn, natural persons submit structured financial statements to the head of the KAS. As the Ministry of Finance supports the creation of structured reports by offering a free tool to facilitate the fulfilment of this obligation, the proposed amendment will facilitate and simplify the information obligation for non-business CIT taxpayers. The second of the proposed amendments, in Article. 27a of the CIT Act, concerns the restoration of the possibility of submitting an annual CIT-8 statement in paper form to those CIT taxpayers whose total income is exempt from income tax. From 1 January this year, pursuant to Article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1c of the CIT Act, the annual statement of CIT-8 taxpayers of this tax shall be submitted by electronic means in accordance with the provisions of the Tax Order. Therefore, any such statement, regardless of the size of the taxpayer and the scope of the action, should take the form of an electronic document bearing an electronic signature. The requirement to have a qualified electronic signature generates additional costs related to its purchase and certification. Given that such costs can be important especially for the smallest taxable persons, who do not employ workers, who are engaged in social and useful activities, i.e. foundations, associations, near the rural hostess, the proposed solution is to enable such non-resident individuals' income tax payers, which achieve only tax-free income, to submit CIT-8's tax returns in paper form without having to bear the above-mentioned costs. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u307	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to present my position on the Government's draft law on the repeal of the Act on special hydrocarbon tax and on the amendment of certain other laws, print No 3718. The draft concerns the repeal of the Act of 25 July 2014 on a special hydrocarbon tax, which at the same time alters the time limit for taxing the extraction of natural gas and crude oil set out in the Act of 2 March 2012 on the extraction of certain minerals. The basis for the special hydrocarbon tax is the profit from the production of hydrocarbons, which is the surplus generated in the year in question by the revenues from the production of hydrocarbons over the eligible expenditure incurred in the tax year in question. The revenue is the money received, the monetary value and the value of the claim regulated in kind. The Act on special hydrocarbon tax was established on the basis of previous assumptions concerning the wealth of Poland in the deposits of unconventional gas. According to the report of the American energy agency, the shale gas deposit in Poland was to be the highest in Europe and amounted to 5.3 trillion m3 of gas. In March 2012, the Council adopted The National Geological Institute presented a report on shale gas resources in the Baltic-sublasco-lubile basin, according to which the mining resources in this pool were to be only 1.92 trillion m3, with documented conventional resources of only 145 billion m3. In November 2015, the Council adopted Decision (CFSP) The National Geological Institute estimated Polish shale gas resources at a lower level, between 346 and 768 billion m3. The shale gas at the time enjoyed a great interest of the entrepreneurs. The number of concessions granted for the exploration and recognition of shale gas increased avalanchely between 2010 and 2013, with a peak of more than 100, exactly 108 concessions. According to information from the Ministry of Environment of April 2017, the number of concessions was only 20 at that time, so the number of concessions in force decreased by more than 81%. There has also been a drastic decline in the dynamics of exploration and exploration of shale gas. Contrary to forecasts, there has been no development in shale gas extraction in Poland, and conventional natural gas deposits remain the primary source of this raw material. The current shale gas deposits do not provide sufficient opportunities for their economic exploitation and the legislator's assumption that the hydrocarbon tax will provide the State budget with an adequate share of the profits of shale gas extraction has not been met. The number of entities making declarations on special hydrocarbon tax falls gradually: from 123 in 2016, this figure has already fallen to 80 in 2017, with the vast majority of taxpayers showing a loss in the declarations. Therefore, in the article. 1 This law introduces a legal solution, which assumes that the Act on special hydrocarbon tax will be repealed. For these obvious reasons, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this bill in further legislative work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u320	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! It is my great honor to present a project aimed at penalising illegal or circumvention of the right to adopt children. I am talking about introducing a solution that shows how to define adoption, because for the purposes of the Penal Code, there has been no definition of what adoption really is for the purposes of criminal law at all. And we have proposed the introduction in the article. 115 The criminal code definition of the legal term adoption, because it is in some sense a legally indeterminate concept. Here, of course, we followed some solutions, some doctrinal clues, and proposed to define adoption as the acquisition of parental authority over the child by a person other than the one from which the child comes. This is the terminology used by the Family and Care Code, precisely in the article. 73 Family and Care Code. Thus, in the light of this standardization, adoption involves all situations where the result of the action is the acquisition of parental authority over the child, regardless of the legal instruments used for this purpose, i.e. recognition of the child, obtaining the decision of the court of adoption, etc. As regards the form of illegal adoption according to the project, in the first case, it is a misconception of the procedure of adoption, i.e. the exercise of the procedure, which is, of course, provided for by the legal provisions, provided by the legislator for the acquisition of parental authority by a person who is not a biological parent. And this course of action is, of course, carried out. They're often long-term, and we know it very well, but why? For a very simple reason. The point is that parents should know what their responsibilities are, that they should be properly checked by the courts, that they deserve to be looked after by the child, but that the child should also get used to the person in question to see how he reacts. Of course there's a whole bunch of challenges here, but that's how the legislator built it to maximize the child's welfare. We recognise the situation in which the perpetrator acts in order to obtain a property or personal advantage and at the same time conceal this fact before the court in the adoption proceedings. We have here, in fact, a situation in which the parties, by getting along, act in order to achieve a personal benefit, i.e. For example, the promotion of a professional or property, that is, they date one another, and they do it in such a way that the court is not able to understand that these parties have gotten along with each other. In fact, the welfare of the child is being avoided and the state is really lying to it. Of course, there are exceptional situations and we also need to remember that. Sometimes a person trying to adopt, for example a person who is not a biological parent, transfers some small amount, diapers, a wheelchair. These are emergency situations. We leave a safety valve for courts, which in any particular situation will determine whether any means, objects of everyday use, which are passed on, fall within this concept of property benefit or personal benefit. I'm talking about different situations. It's about whether or not you've got to get that kind of mentalized. If the family court considers that they are not significant, then it will not affect the purpose of the adoption procedure itself, so there will be no social harm to such behaviour. And there's a second situation. It is about adopting without adopting, i.e. when the perpetrator uses other legal institutions to achieve the effect of adopting or giving up parental rights by the mother for the purpose of such adoption by another person. This is especially about the first situation. Ladies and gentlemen, the Polish system has a huge gap today. There may be a situation where the child's birth mother comes to the civil status office, puts herself before the head of the civil status office and says that the other person, the man, is the biological father of the child, which is not true, or lies. I would like to tell you that this situation is practically unpunished. See what happens next. They're making a statement of their will. The head of the civil status office does not have any instrumentarium to examine whether it is true or not, even if he has suspicions, ladies and gentlemen. And that's the big problem. What's going on next? In the following weeks, the child's birth mother renounces power to this child. And this full authority is acquired by someone who is not the biological father of a child, even though it is in the records. He's going abroad. We can't protect the child in this situation. Anyone with imagination can ask himself what can happen next, where the state protects the child. After all, this is an excellent gap, and I stress it very strongly that criminals use it. I understand there are very different situations in life. It's obvious. There may be a situation in which someone is simply unable to go through this adoption procedure, e.g. He thinks it's too long. ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u326	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! I would like to present the position of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party - Coalition Poland on the draft law on amending the Law of the Penal Code contained in the sejm print No 3665. In the contested bill, the government has set itself the objective of penalising illegal or circumvention children's adoption rights. Current legal solutions do not sufficiently protect children from the threat of getting under the actual care of the wrong people. Criminal liability today only threatens the organisation of large-scale illegal adoptions. The dangerous phenomenon has expanded to such an extent that state centers dealing with finding families for single children alert about the existence of a grey adoption zone in Poland. On the Internet you can easily find announcements of women who offer birthing a child and giving it right after giving birth. According to estimates in Poland it can reach up to 2 000 per year. cases of actual trafficking in children at ca. 3 000 legal adoption cases. According to the provisions of the draft law, an illegal adoption takes place when the perpetrator surrenders or adopts the child for adoption in order to obtain a personal or property benefit and conceals that fact before the court in the adoption proceedings. The point is that the court does not know that the parties have reached an agreement on the benefit of biological parents, and it is kept secret from the court to make it seem that their aim is only the welfare of the child. If the court knows this, it may take the right decision and, as a rule, refuse to adopt as requested. However, it is not necessary to punish the mere surrender or adoption of the child in order to achieve a personal or property benefit, as it may turn out that despite the handover of small gratification, e.g. a special gift, the child's well-being will not suffer, and the accepters are trustworthy persons. The court must know and decide to adopt, given this fact. Another form of illegal adoption is the situation where the perpetrator uses other legal institutions to achieve the effect of adoption, e.g. Fictitious recognition of the child and giving up parental rights by the mother. It is possible to agree with the criminalisation of the surrender or adoption of the child without adopting the adoption procedure, but to object to the inclusion in that provision of any such accident, even if the perpetrator does not act in order to achieve a financial or personal advantage, since it is, in fact, a measure aimed at achieving the same effect as the conduct described in point 1 of the proposed Article. 211a § 2. The explanatory memorandum of the bill explicitly stresses that the second form of illegal adoption is to do so without adopting, i.e. when the perpetrator uses other legal institutions to achieve the effect of adoption, e.g. Fictitious recognition of the child and giving up parental rights by the mother. This form of conduct should be criminalised only if the perpetrator acts to obtain a property or personal benefit. In addition, the criminal threat provided for in the proposed Article raises objections. 211a § 2 k.k., which is identical to the sanction provided for in § 1 concerning the handling of illegal adoption in order to achieve a property benefit, i.e. the sentence of imprisonment from 3 months to 5 years. However, the procedure for organising illegal adoptions is far more dangerous. By the way, it should also be noted that the number of adoptions in Poland, which remained at a similar level in the past years, is unfortunately steadily decreasing. In 2018, the fall in the number of adoptions was almost 21% compared to 2015. This is a phenomenon that is deeply worrying and which is not included in the family policy of the current government. In view of the fact that this is an amendment to the Code, pursuant to Article. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, second reading of the project referred to in Article. 87. 1, may take place not earlier than 14 days from the date of delivery to Members of the Extraordinary Commissions report. Despite some reservations, the PSL parliamentary club is in favour of continuing its work on this project. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2019-08-30-sejm-85-3.u340	After the work of the Committee on Culture and Communications has been completed, the final proposal presented to the High House is as follows: The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland commemorating the victims of the Pomeranian crime of 1939. As a result of World War I and the provisions of the Treaty of Versailles, the western lands, including the Pomerania of Gdańsk, returned to the reborn Poland. The German state and society did not accept this fact and the Second Republic treated it as a seasonal state. After the German invasion of Poland on 1 September 1939, the German authorities joined the 'depreciation' of Polish lands, which were directly attached to the Third Reich. To this end, mass shootings of the Polish civilian population, which was considered an anti-German element, including women and children, began on the Polish Western Kreses. The purpose of the German invader's aggression was first Polish intelligence. The biggest and most brutal crimes committed in the first months of the occupation occurred in the pre-war Pomeranian province with the capital in Toruń, the so-called. The Great Pomerania. About 30,000 people were murdered in more than 400 towns, sandboxes, forests and death pits. Polish patriots, who were actively involved in Poland's regaining independence and strengthening Polishness in Pomerania, were particularly affected by the repression. The Pomeranian Jews were also shot. In the Pomerania pits of death, the Germans also killed patients of Polish and German psychiatric hospitals. The largest Pomeranian places of execution were: Piśnica near Wejherów, Szpęgawsk near Starogard Gdański, Lasa Kaliskine next to Kartuz, Skarszewy, Mniszek near Świecia nad Wisła, Needles in Chojnice, Rudzki Bridge near Tuchola, Karolewo and Radzim near Sępólna Krajeńskiego, Paterek near Nakł nad Notecią, Tryszczyn and Fordońska Valley of Death near Bydgoszcz, Barbarka near Toruń, Clamry near Chełmn, Łopatka near Wąbrzeźn and Skrwilno near Rypin. The main perpetrators of genocide were members of German police and security forces (Einsatzgruppen) and Selbstschutz Westpreussen, which included the local Germans - pre-war neighbours of Poles. It is worth remembering, however, that not all Polish citizens of German origin engaged in the extermination of Poles and that among them were also those who tried to help their Polish neighbours. The total number of victims of the 1939 Pomeranian crime is impossible to establish due to the destruction of the documentation and the cover-up of the crime. In 1944. The Germans extracted the bodies of victims murdered at the beginning of the occupation from many Pomerania pits and then burned them. The vast majority of the perpetrators have avoided criminal liability. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, bearing in mind these tragic events, pays tribute to all victims of the Pomeranian crime and honors their memory. As I said at the outset, the Committee on Culture and Media asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft resolution to pay tribute to all victims of the Pomeranian crime and to honor the memory of Polish patriots. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I have the honour to present a bill on ratification of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the United States of America on cooperation in the field of border security and immigration, signed in Washington on 16 August 2019. High Izbo! The purpose of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the United States of America on cooperation in the field of border security and immigration, signed in Washington on 16 August 2019, is to provide mutual assistance in the effective application and enforcement of their national provisions applicable to persons arriving in, residing in and leaving their territories, through the exchange of personal data and additional information concerning those persons. This cooperation is to be carried out primarily by exchanging information to determine or verify the identity of a person, including his nationality or country of origin, and by establishing possible grounds for refusal of departure, entry and stay and the reasons for the person's obligation to return. The agreement contains solutions for access to biometric databases, determines how other personal data and additional information are transmitted and regulates the protection of personal data transferred. The arrangements provided for in the agreement will undoubtedly increase the level of security and public order in both countries. The Polish Party is interested in binding an agreement on cooperation in the field of border security and immigration, because its provisions will enable the prevention and prevention of illegal immigration by checking compliance with the rules on the entry and stay of foreigners in the territory of the Republic of Poland and by identifying and analysing migratory threats. The Agreement will form the legal basis for the transfer to the United States of a third country within the meaning of the Directive of the European Parliament of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data by competent authorities for the purposes of the prevention, investigation, detection and prosecution of criminal offences and the enforcement of penalties, on the free movement of such data, of personal data. These data may include biometric data, photographs and other information. As these issues constitute statutory matter and relate to freedoms and civil rights, the conditions laid down in Article are therefore met. Article 89 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 points 2 and 5 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the binding of the Republic of Poland by the agreement in question should take place in the form of ratification in favour of the previous agreement expressed in the Act, which is in accordance with the Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of 14 April 2000 on international agreements. This agreement is very similar and very similar to another agreement, which was signed quite recently, because on 12 June 2019. It concerned strengthening cooperation in the field of prevention and combating serious crime and was signed, as I mentioned, on 12 June 2019. Today, on behalf of the Government, I request that the Act on ratification of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the United States of America on cooperation in the field of border security and immigration, signed in Washington on 16 August 2019. Questions concerning contact points were asked by the corresponding law. This applies to persons residing in the country concerned. This cooperation is to be carried out by exchanging information to identify or verify the identity of a person, including his nationality or country of origin, establishing possible grounds for refusal of entry, residence or reasons for the person's obligation to return. Effective application of the rules on the entry and residence of persons in and exit from the territory of a Party State will contribute to increasing the level of security and public order in both countries. The Agreement concerns natural persons to the extent that they will be subject to border controls or will be subject to administrative proceedings against them as aliens. In such a situation, personal data of natural persons may be transferred in accordance with the contract. The implementation of the agreement will mainly concern the bodies subordinate to the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration, including the Chief of the Border Guard, the Chief Police Commissioner and the Central Police Police Laboratory and the Head of the Office for Foreigners. The conclusion by the Republic of Poland of the agreement will not result in changes in national law. High Izbo! The Law and Justice Club supports the draft law in its entirety. Thank you very much.
